青海省民族自治州中小学生监护人接种人乳头瘤病毒疫苗的意愿及影响因素——基于健康信念模型和计划行为理论的横断面研究

Guardians’ willingness to vaccinate primary and secondary school girls against human papillomavirus and associated factors in ethnic autonomous prefectures of Qinghai Province, China: A cross-sectional study based on the Health Belief Model and Theory of Planned Behavior
作者： Xiao-Lin Li, Xiao-Qing Liu, Gui-Yan Ma, Lei Wang, Guang Hu, Hai-Lian Yuan, Kezhong A, A K, K. A
摘要中文翻译宫颈癌 是对女性健康的可预防威胁,而 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 疫苗接种 是关键的一级预防。照顾者的犹豫可能会影响适龄女童的疫苗接种。我们调查了青海省六个民族自治州的照顾者为中小学生接种HPV疫苗的意愿及其影响因素。从 2024 年 12 月到 2025 年 2 月,我们采用多阶段整群便利抽样方法,对 12 所学校选定班级的女童照顾者进行了横断面调查。问卷包括健康信念模型(HBM)、计划行为理论(TPB)和感知社会支持量表(PSSS)项目。采用单变量和四步分层多元线性回归评估因素,依次进入社会人口统计学、HBM、TPB 和 PSSS 模块。意愿与感知的易感性和严重性、行动线索、态度、感知的行为控制和主观规范相关。在单变量分析中,除感知障碍外,所有 HBM、TPB 和社会支持维度均与意愿呈正相关。回族照顾者的意愿较低,而高等教育、中等收入、党政机关或事业单位就业预示着较高的意愿。在层次多元回归中,回族与汉族的预测意愿较低(B = -0.229,P = .003)。行动线索(β = 0.121,P = .024)、感知障碍(β = 0.128,P < .001)、态度(β = 0.203,P < .001)、感知行为控制(β = 0.107,P = .001)和主观规范(β = 0.323,P < .001)呈正相关。HPV 疫苗接种 推广应针对青海民族自治州量身定制,有权威的学校和基层医疗建议、清晰的 疫苗 信息、疫苗接种 流程支持,同时识别障碍。未来的定性和前瞻性实施研究应阐明决策机制,并评估干预措施的可接受性以及对资源有限环境中 疫苗 吸收的影响。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究调查了中国青海省六个民族自治州的看护者为中小学生接种 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 疫苗的意愿及相关因素。核心问题是关于 疫苗接种 意愿的决定因素,重点是社会人口、心理和社会因素。
证据基础与方法
该研究是一项横断面调查,于 2024 年 12 月至 2025 年 2 月期间进行,采用多阶段整群便利抽样。问卷纳入了健康信念模型(HBM)、计划行为理论(TPB)和感知社会支持量表(PSSS)。使用单变量和分层多元线性回归分析因素,并按顺序输入社会人口统计学、HBM、TPB 和 PSSS 模块。
共识与结论
接种疫苗的意愿与感知的易感性、严重程度、行动线索、态度、感知的行为控制和主观规范相关。与汉族照顾者相比,回族照顾者的意愿较低,而高等教育、中等收入以及在党政机关或公共机构工作的人则预示着较高的意愿。感知到的障碍也与意愿呈正相关。
争议与缺口
该研究没有报告 疫苗 犹豫的具体机制或解决这些因素的干预措施的有效性。它还没有探讨文化或宗教信仰在塑造疫苗接种意愿中的作用,这可能是民族自治区的一个重要决定因素。
科研与实践启示
HPV 疫苗接种 推广应针对青海民族自治州量身定制,结合权威的学校和基层医疗建议、明确的 疫苗 信息以及对 疫苗接种 流程的支持。未来的定性和前瞻性实施研究应阐明决策机制,并评估干预措施的可接受性以及对资源有限环境中 疫苗 吸收的影响。

Guardians’ willingness to vaccinate primary and secondary school girls against human papillomavirus and associated factors in ethnic autonomous prefectures of Qinghai Province, China: A cross-sectional study based on the Health Belief Model and Theory of Planned Behavior

Authors: Xiao-Lin Li, Xiao-Qing Liu, Gui-Yan Ma, Lei Wang, Guang Hu, Hai-Lian Yuan, Kezhong A, A K, K. A
Original abstract (English)
Cervical cancer is a preventable threat to women's health, and human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination is key primary prevention. Caregiver hesitancy may affect vaccination of age-eligible girls.We examined caregivers' willingness to vaccinate primary and secondary school girls against HPV and factors in six ethnic autonomous prefectures of Qinghai Province. From December 2024 to February 2025, we conducted a cross-sectional survey of caregivers of girls in selected classes at 12 schools, using multistage cluster convenience sampling. The questionnaire included Health Belief Model (HBM), Theory of Planned Behavior (TPB), and Perceived Social Support Scale (PSSS) items. Factors were assessed with univariable and four-step hierarchical multiple linear regression, sequentially entering sociodemographic, HBM, TPB, and PSSS blocks. Willingness correlated with perceived susceptibility and severity, cues to action, attitudes, perceived behavioral control, and subjective norms. In univariable analyses, all HBM, TPB, and social-support dimensions except perceived barriers were positively associated with willingness. Hui caregivers had lower willingness, while higher education, middle income, and party/government-agency or public-institution employment predicted higher willingness. In hierarchical multiple regression, Hui ethnicity vs. Han ethnicity predicted lower willingness (B = -0.229, P = .003). Cues to action (β = 0.121, P = .024), perceived barriers (β = 0.128, P < .001), attitudes (β = 0.203, P < .001), perceived behavioral control (β = 0.107, P = .001), and subjective norms (β = 0.323, P < .001) were positively associated. HPV vaccination promotion should be tailored to Qinghai's ethnic autonomous prefectures, with authoritative school and primary-care recommendations, clear vaccine information, and vaccination-process support, while identifying barriers. Future qualitative and prospective implementation studies should clarify decision-making mechanisms and assess intervention acceptability and effects on vaccine uptake in resource-limited settings.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study examines caregivers' willingness to vaccinate primary and secondary school girls against human papillomavirus (HPV) and associated factors in six ethnic autonomous prefectures of Qinghai Province, China. The central question is about the determinants of vaccination willingness, with a focus on sociodemographic, psychological, and social factors.
Evidence base and method
The study is a cross-sectional survey conducted from December 2024 to February 2025, using multistage cluster convenience sampling. The questionnaire incorporated the Health Belief Model (HBM), Theory of Planned Behavior (TPB), and Perceived Social Support Scale (PSSS). Factors were analyzed using univariable and hierarchical multiple linear regression, with sociodemographic, HBM, TPB, and PSSS blocks entered sequentially.
Consensus and conclusions
Willingness to vaccinate correlated with perceived susceptibility, severity, cues to action, attitudes, perceived behavioral control, and subjective norms. Hui caregivers had lower willingness compared to Han caregivers, and higher education, middle income, and employment in party/government-agency or public-institution settings predicted higher willingness. Perceived barriers were also positively associated with willingness.
Controversies and gaps
The study did not report on the specific mechanisms of vaccine hesitancy or the effectiveness of interventions to address these factors. It also did not explore the role of cultural or religious beliefs in shaping vaccination willingness, which could be a significant determinant in ethnic autonomous regions.
Research and practice implications
HPV vaccination promotion should be tailored to Qinghai's ethnic autonomous prefectures, incorporating authoritative school and primary-care recommendations, clear vaccine information, and support for the vaccination process. Future qualitative and prospective implementation studies should clarify decision-making mechanisms and assess intervention acceptability and effects on vaccine uptake in resource-limited settings.

成人女性血小板-淋巴细胞比值和淋巴细胞与人乳头瘤病毒感染的关系:一项横断面分析

Association of platelet-to-lymphocyte ratio and lymphocyte with HPV infection in adult women: a cross-sectional analysis
作者： Mei Wei, Rui-Rui Yu, Zheng’ai Xiong, Yuanyuan Hua
摘要中文翻译背景:人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)感染是全球重要的公共卫生问题。炎症指标与多种疾病状况相关。然而,血小板-淋巴细胞比值(PLR)、血小板(PLT)和淋巴细胞(LYM)与HPV感染之间的关系尚不明确。本研究旨在探讨PLR、PLT和LYM与HPV感染之间的关系。方法:本项横断面研究使用了2011年至2016年国家健康与营养调查(NHANES)的数据。PLR计算为PLT/LYM。采用多变量逻辑回归分析评估PLR与HPV感染之间的关联。使用受试者工作特征(ROC)曲线计算曲线下面积(AUC)。采用限制性立方样条(RCS)分析评估LYM与HPV感染之间的关联,并进行了亚组分析和交互作用检验。结果:本研究纳入了5,415名成年女性,其中2,301名患有HPV感染,3,114名未感染。当将PLR作为连续变量分析时,多变量逻辑回归显示其与HPV感染存在显著的负相关(比值比[OR] = 0.99)。将PLR分为四分位数后,最高PLR四分位数(Q4: 143.70-477.14)的参与者与最低四分位数(Q1: 12.86-92.60)的参与者相比,HPV感染的比值比更低。模型I、II和III的AUC值分别为0.53、0.64和0.72。未观察到PLT与HPV感染之间存在显著关联。相反,LYM与HPV感染之间存在显著正相关,RCS分析表明其关系呈线性趋势。交互作用分析表明,这种关联会根据PIR而变化。结论:较高的PLR与较低的HPV感染风险相关,而较高的LYM水平与较高的HPV感染风险相关。这些发现为炎症指标与HPV感染之间的关系提供了额外的证据。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)是全球重要的公共卫生问题。本研究旨在探讨血小板-淋巴细胞比值(PLR)、血小板(PLT)和淋巴细胞(LYM)水平与成人女性HPV感染之间的关系。
证据基础与方法
这项横断面研究使用了 2011 年至 2016 年国家健康和营养检查调查 (NHANES) 的数据。PLR 计算为 PLT/LYM。进行多变量逻辑回归来评估 PLR 与 HPV 感染之间的关联。受试者工作特征(ROC)曲线用于计算曲线下面积(AUC)。应用限制三次样条 (RCS) 分析评估 LYM 与 HPV 感染之间的关联,并进行亚组分析和相互作用检验。
共识与结论
较高的血小板与淋巴细胞比率(PLR)与较低的 HPV 感染几率相关,而较高的淋巴细胞(LYM)水平与较高的 HPV 感染几率相关。模型 I、II 和 III 的 AUC 值分别为 0.53、0.64 和 0.72。LYM 和 HPV 感染之间的关联显示出线性关系,交互分析表明这种关联根据 PIR 的不同而变化。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据并未报告与 PLR、PLT 和 LYM 与 HPV 感染之间的关联相关的任何争议或证据差距。
科研与实践启示
所提供的证据未报告与PLR、PLT和LYM与HPV感染关联相关的具体研究优先事项、流行病学、生态学、风险评估或政策。

Association of platelet-to-lymphocyte ratio and lymphocyte with HPV infection in adult women: a cross-sectional analysis

Authors: Mei Wei, Rui-Rui Yu, Zheng’ai Xiong, Yuanyuan Hua
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Human papillomavirus (HPV) infection represents a major public health concern worldwide. Inflammatory indicators have been associated with various disease conditions. However, the associations of platelet-to-lymphocyte ratio (PLR), platelet (PLT), and lymphocyte (LYM) with HPV infection remain unclear. This study aimed to investigate the relationships between PLR, PLT, LYM, and HPV infection. METHODS: This cross-sectional study used data from the National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey (NHANES) from 2011 to 2016. PLR was calculated as PLT/LYM. Multivariable logistic regression was performed to evaluate the associations between PLR and HPV infection. Receiver operating characteristic (ROC) curves were used to calculate the area under the curve (AUC). Restricted cubic spline (RCS) analysis was applied to assess the association between LYM and HPV infection, and subgroup analyses with interaction tests were conducted. RESULTS: Among 5,415 adult females included in this study, 2,301 had HPV infection, and 3,114 did not. When PLR was analysed as a continuous variable, multivariable logistic regression showed a significant inverse association with HPV infection (odds ratio [OR] = 0.99). After categorising PLR into quartiles, participants in the highest PLR quartile (Q4: 143.70-477.14) showed lower odds of HPV infection compared with those in the lowest quartile (Q1: 12.86-92.60). The AUC values were 0.53, 0.64, and 0.72 for models I, II, and III, respectively. No significant association was observed between PLT and HPV infection. In contrast, LYM showed a significant positive association with HPV infection, with RCS analysis indicating a linear relationship. Interaction analysis suggested that this association varied according to PIR. CONCLUSION: Higher PLR was associated with lower odds of HPV infection, whereas higher LYM levels were associated with higher odds of HPV infection. These findings provide additional evidence regarding the associations between inflammatory indicators and HPV infection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Human papillomaviruses (HPV) are a major public health concern worldwide. This study aimed to investigate the relationships between platelet-to-lymphocyte ratio (PLR), platelet (PLT), and lymphocyte (LYM) levels and HPV infection in adult women.
Evidence base and method
This cross-sectional study used data from the National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey (NHANES) from 2011 to 2016. PLR was calculated as PLT/LYM. Multivariable logistic regression was performed to evaluate the associations between PLR and HPV infection. Receiver operating characteristic (ROC) curves were used to calculate the area under the curve (AUC). Restricted cubic spline (RCS) analysis was applied to assess the association between LYM and HPV infection, and subgroup analyses with interaction tests were conducted.
Consensus and conclusions
Higher platelet-to-lymphocyte ratio (PLR) was associated with lower odds of HPV infection, whereas higher lymphocyte (LYM) levels were associated with higher odds of HPV infection. The AUC values were 0.53, 0.64, and 0.72 for models I, II, and III, respectively. The association between LYM and HPV infection showed a linear relationship, and interaction analysis suggested that this association varied according to PIR.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report any controversies or evidence gaps related to the associations between PLR, PLT, and LYM with HPV infection.
Research and practice implications
The supplied evidence does not report specific priorities for research, surveillance, diagnosis, treatment, vaccination, ecology, risk assessment, or policy related to the associations between PLR, PLT, and LYM with HPV infection.

FIGO/IGCS关于局部应用治疗对高危型人乳头瘤病毒感染预防宫颈上皮内瘤变3(CIN 3+)病变的效力声明

FIGO/IGCS Statement on the efficacy of topical treatments for high-risk human papillomavirus in preventing CIN 3+ lesions
作者： René Pareja, Fernando Martínez Heredia, Emmanuel Sánchez-Díaz, Mauricio A. Cuello, Silvia de Sanjosé, José Jerónimo, Suzanne M. Garland, Marta del Pino, Angélica Fletcher, Aureli Torné
摘要中文翻译摘要 宫颈癌 是由持续高危 人乳头瘤病毒 (HR-HPV) 感染引起的,尽管有现有的预防策略,但它仍然是全球主要的健康负担。大多数 HR-HPV 感染和相关的低度鳞状上皮内病变 (LSIL) 会自发消退,但临床实践中已经出现了针对 HPV 清除的局部治疗的商业推广不断增加。该FIGO/国际妇科癌症协会 (IGCS) 立场声明严格评估了局部 治疗 针对 HR-HPV 感染和 LSIL 的现有证据,并为临床实践和未来研究提供了基于证据的建议。目前还没有充分设计或有效的随机对照试验来评估局部治疗对预防组织学证实的高级鳞状上皮内病变 (HSIL/CIN3+) 的效果。现有研究的特点是样本量小、随访期短、人群异质、比较器不足以及依赖临床相关性不确定的替代终点,包括细胞学回归、HPV未检出和免疫组织化学标记物,例如p16/Ki-67。已发表的荟萃分析报告了病毒学和细胞学替代指标在统计​​学上的显着改善,但受到显着异质性、发表偏倚和不到 1 年的随访期的限制。对云芝和二氧化硅/亚硒酸盐阴道凝胶的产品特定评估揭示了其他方法学问题,包括回顾性试验注册、行业资助以及对与旋转现象一致的研究结果的误解。在缺乏高质量证据证明具有临床意义的益处的情况下,FIGO 和 IGCS 不认可任何目前可用的局部用药治疗 HR-HPV 感染或 LSIL 的常规临床使用。循证 监测 仍然是护理标准。未来的研究应优先考虑严格的试验设计,以组织学证实的 HSIL/CIN3+ 作为主要终点,至少 3 年的充分随访,以及适当的比较。临床管理应以知情的共同决策和循证咨询为中心,而不是缺乏经过证实的临床益处的干预措施。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述关注局部应用治疗对高危型人乳头瘤病毒(HR-HPV)感染预防宫颈上皮内瘤变3(CIN 3+)病变的效力。核心问题是这些治疗是否能有效预防与HR-HPV相关的高级别鳞状上皮内病变(HSIL/CIN3+).
证据基础与方法
本综述评估了针对HR-HPV感染和低级别鳞状上皮内病变(LSIL)的局部应用治疗的研究。这些研究的特点包括样本量小、随访时间短、人群异质性高、对照不足以及依赖替代终点,如细胞学退行、HPV未检出以及免疫组化标志物如p16/Ki-67。目前尚无设计或样本量充足的研究能够评估局部治疗对预防组织学确诊的HSIL/CIN3+的效果。
共识与结论
综述得出结论,目前没有高质量的证据表明局部应用治疗对HR-HPV感染或LSIL具有临床意义的益处。现有的荟萃分析受到显著异质性、发表偏倚以及随访时间不足1年的限制。因此,FIGO/IGCS立场声明不支持目前任何局部药物在HR-HPV感染或LSIL中的常规临床应用。
争议与缺口
证据具有异质性,存在相互矛盾的结果以及方法学上的问题,如样本量小、随访时间短和依赖替代终点。此外,基于Coriolus versicolor和二氧化硅/硒酸盐阴道凝胶的产品特定研究引发了诸如回顾性试验注册、行业资助以及与“spin phenomenon”一致的结果误读等问题。缺乏以组织学确诊的HSIL/CIN3+作为主要终点且随访时间至少为3年的研究。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先考虑严格的试验设计,以组织学证实的 HSIL/CIN3+ 作为主要终点,至少 3 年的充分随访,以及适当的比较。循证 监测 仍然是护理标准,临床管理应侧重于知情的共同决策和循证咨询,而不是没有经过证实的临床益处的干预措施。

FIGO/IGCS Statement on the efficacy of topical treatments for high-risk human papillomavirus in preventing CIN 3+ lesions

Authors: René Pareja, Fernando Martínez Heredia, Emmanuel Sánchez-Díaz, Mauricio A. Cuello, Silvia de Sanjosé, José Jerónimo, Suzanne M. Garland, Marta del Pino, Angélica Fletcher, Aureli Torné
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Cervical cancer is caused by persistent high‐risk human papillomavirus (HR‐HPV) infection and remains a major global health burden despite available preventive strategies. Most HR‐HPV infections and associated low‐grade squamous intraepithelial lesions (LSIL) regress spontaneously, yet increasing commercial promotion of topical therapies targeting HPV clearance has emerged in clinical practice. This FIGO/International Gynecologic Cancer Society (IGCS) Position Statement critically appraises the available evidence on topical treatments for HR‐HPV infection and LSIL, and provides evidence‐based recommendations for clinical practice and future research. No randomized controlled trial has been adequately designed or powered to evaluate the effect of topical therapies on the prevention of histologically confirmed high‐grade squamous intraepithelial lesions (HSIL/CIN3+). Available studies are characterized by small sample sizes, short follow‐up periods, heterogeneous populations, inadequate comparators, and reliance on surrogate endpoints of uncertain clinical relevance, including cytological regression, HPV nondetection, and immunohistochemical markers such as p16/Ki‐67. Published meta‐analyses reporting statistically significant improvements in virological and cytological surrogates are limited by substantial heterogeneity, publication bias, and follow‐up periods under 1 year. Product‐specific appraisal of Coriolus versicolor‐based and silicon dioxide/selenite‐based vaginal gels reveal additional methodological concerns, including retrospective trial registration, industry funding, and misinterpretation of findings consistent with the spin phenomenon. In the absence of high‐quality evidence demonstrating clinically meaningful benefit, FIGO and IGCS do not endorse the routine clinical use of any currently available topical agent for HR‐HPV infection or LSIL. Evidence‐based surveillance remains the standard of care. Future research should prioritize rigorous trial design with histologically confirmed HSIL/CIN3+ as the primary endpoint, adequate follow‐up of at least 3 years, and appropriate comparators. Clinical management should center on informed shared decision‐making and evidence‐based counseling, rather than on interventions lacking proven clinical benefit.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the efficacy of topical treatments for high-risk human papillomavirus (HR-HPV) infection in preventing cervical intraepithelial neoplasia 3 (CIN 3+) lesions. The central question is whether these treatments can effectively prevent HR-HPV-related high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions (HSIL/CIN3+).
Evidence base and method
The review evaluates available studies on topical treatments for HR-HPV infection and low-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions (LSIL). These studies are characterized by small sample sizes, short follow-up periods, heterogeneous populations, inadequate comparators, and reliance on surrogate endpoints such as cytological regression, HPV nondetection, and immunohistochemical markers like p16/Ki-67. No randomized controlled trial has been adequately designed or powered to assess the effect of topical therapies on the prevention of histologically confirmed HSIL/CIN3+.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that there is no high-quality evidence demonstrating clinically meaningful benefit from topical treatments for HR-HPV infection or LSIL. The available meta-analyses are limited by substantial heterogeneity, publication bias, and follow-up periods under 1 year. Therefore, the FIGO/IGCS Position Statement does not endorse the routine clinical use of any currently available topical agent for HR-HPV infection or LSIL.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence is heterogeneous, with conflicting findings and methodological concerns such as small sample sizes, short follow-up periods, and reliance on surrogate endpoints. Additionally, product-specific studies on Coriolus versicolor-based and silicon dioxide/selenite-based vaginal gels raise issues like retrospective trial registration, industry funding, and misinterpretation of findings consistent with the spin phenomenon. There is a lack of studies with histologically confirmed HSIL/CIN3+ as the primary endpoint and adequate follow-up of at least 3 years.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize rigorous trial design with histologically confirmed HSIL/CIN3+ as the primary endpoint, adequate follow-up of at least 3 years, and appropriate comparators. Evidence-based surveillance remains the standard of care, and clinical management should focus on informed shared decision-making and evidence-based counseling rather than on interventions without proven clinical benefit.

人乳头瘤病毒循环肿瘤DNA在局部晚期宫颈癌患者治疗监测和复发预测中的临床应用:系统综述

Clinical Utility of Human Papillomavirus ctDNA for Treatment Monitoring and Recurrence Prediction in Locally Advanced Cervical Cancer: A Systematic Review
作者： Dilfuza Mirzaeva, Sharodiya Das, Laziz Y. T. O'G'Li, Djurayeva Z. Baratovna, Dilshod Tolibov, Xalmuxamedov B. Taxirovich, Dilorom Dinmukhammadieva, Sourav Bhattacharjee, Дилфуза Мирзаева, Дилором Динмухаммадиева
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述关注人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)循环肿瘤DNA(ctDNA)在局部晚期宫颈癌(LACC)患者治疗监测和复发预测中的临床应用。核心问题是HPV ctDNA是否可以作为检测微小残留病(MRD)和预测确定性化学放疗(CRT)后复发的可靠生物标志物。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对2017年至2026年间从Scopus和PubMed数据库中检索到的研究进行的系统分析。它包括七篇论文,探讨了在进行放化疗(CRT)的局部晚期宫颈癌(LACC)患者中,人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)ctDNA作为分子标志物的纵向检测。研究分析了诸如微残留病(MRD)、复发和生存等结局,利用了诸如液滴数字聚合酶链反应和下一代测序等敏感的分子技术。
共识与结论
HPV ctDNA 阳性与较差的无进展生存期相关,风险比范围为 2.53 至 14.25。它还可以提前 10 个月预测放射学复发。治疗 结束时或 CRT 后 3 个月时 HPV ctDNA 的持续存在始终与较高的复发风险相关,表明其作为 MRD 检测和早期复发预测的生物标志物的潜力。
争议与缺口
目前监测 LACC 的策略依赖于体检和影像学检查,这可能会受到辐射诱发的纤维化的影响。现有的生物标志物如鳞状细胞癌抗原在检测 MRD 方面缺乏敏感性。该综述强调需要进一步研究来验证 HPV ctDNA 在不同人群中的临床效用并解决方法学局限性。
科研与实践启示
显然需要进行更多研究来评估 HPV ctDNA 在不同患者群体中的有效性,并完善其在临床实践中的使用。研究还应侧重于提高敏感性和特异性,以及将 HPV ctDNA 检测纳入 LACC 患者的常规监测方案中。

Clinical Utility of Human Papillomavirus ctDNA for Treatment Monitoring and Recurrence Prediction in Locally Advanced Cervical Cancer: A Systematic Review

Authors: Dilfuza Mirzaeva, Sharodiya Das, Laziz Y. T. O'G'Li, Djurayeva Z. Baratovna, Dilshod Tolibov, Xalmuxamedov B. Taxirovich, Dilorom Dinmukhammadieva, Sourav Bhattacharjee, Дилфуза Мирзаева, Дилором Динмухаммадиева
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Locally advanced cervical cancer (LACC), defined as FIGO 2018 stages IB3-IVA, is associated with relapse rates between 30 and 60% after definitive chemoradiotherapy (CRT). The current strategy for monitoring is based on physical examinations and imaging, which could be confounded by radiation-induced fibrosis, and existing biomarkers like squamous cell carcinoma antigen lack sensitivity in detecting minimal residual disease (MRD). Circulating tumor DNA derived from human papillomavirus (HPV ctDNA) has recently been shown to be useful for molecular surveillance through liquid biopsies. This article aims to investigate the clinical significance of HPV ctDNA in monitoring the response to therapy and predicting recurrence in patients with LACC. This study is a systematic review of Scopus and PubMed databases (2017–2026) of articles examining the longitudinal detection of HPV ctDNA as a molecular marker in patients with LACC undergoing definitive CRT. Studies utilizing sensitive molecular techniques such as droplet digital polymerase chain reaction and next-generation sequencing were included and analyzed outcomes including MRD, recurrence, and survival. A total of seven papers were selected, utilizing results from large-scale cohort studies, such as CALLA Phase III trial and BioRAIDs trial conducted in Europe. The detection rate of HPV ctDNA at the time of diagnosis was 60.8 to 98.9%, correlating with tumor burden, defined by FIGO staging and nodal disease. The persistence of ctDNA at the end of treatment or 3 months following CRT as evidence of MRD consistently indicated a worse prognosis in terms of relapse. ctDNA positivity predicted worse progression-free survival (hazard ratio: 2.53–14.25) and predated radiographic relapse by 5.5 to 10 months. HPV ctDNA may serve as a promising adjunctive biomarker for MRD detection and early relapse prediction in LACC.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the clinical utility of human papillomavirus (HPV) circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) for treatment monitoring and recurrence prediction in patients with locally advanced cervical cancer (LACC). The central question is whether HPV ctDNA can serve as a reliable biomarker for detecting minimal residual disease (MRD) and predicting relapse following definitive chemoradiotherapy (CRT).
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic analysis of studies retrieved from Scopus and PubMed databases between 2017 and 2026. It includes seven papers that examine the longitudinal detection of HPV ctDNA as a molecular marker in patients with LACC undergoing CRT. The studies analyzed outcomes such as MRD, recurrence, and survival, utilizing sensitive molecular techniques like droplet digital polymerase chain reaction and next-generation sequencing.
Consensus and conclusions
HPV ctDNA positivity was associated with worse progression-free survival, with hazard ratios ranging from 2.53 to 14.25. It also predicted radiographic relapse up to 10 months earlier. The persistence of HPV ctDNA at the end of treatment or 3 months post-CRT was consistently linked to a higher risk of relapse, indicating its potential as a biomarker for MRD detection and early recurrence prediction.
Controversies and gaps
The current strategy for monitoring LACC relies on physical exams and imaging, which may be confounded by radiation-induced fibrosis. Existing biomarkers like squamous cell carcinoma antigen lack sensitivity in detecting MRD. The review highlights the need for further research to validate the clinical utility of HPV ctDNA in diverse populations and to address methodological limitations.
Research and practice implications
There is a clear need for additional studies to evaluate the effectiveness of HPV ctDNA in different patient populations and to refine its use in clinical practice. Research should also focus on improving sensitivity and specificity, as well as integrating HPV ctDNA testing into routine monitoring protocols for LACC patients.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

泌尿科 人乳头瘤病毒 相关疾病:诊断、治疗、预防和医疗保健提供者的临床注意事项

Human Papillomavirus-Related Diseases in Urology: Diagnosis, Treatment, Prevention, and Clinical Considerations for Healthcare Providers
作者： Kyu Won Lee, Amit Mani Upadhyay, Sangrak Bae
摘要中文翻译人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 长期以来被认为是一种妇科病原体,但其在男性中的疾病负担却很大且迅速增加。在韩国,过去十年中男性肛门生殖器疣的 发病率 增加了两倍,阴茎癌持续上升,男性 HPV 阳性口咽癌 (OPC) 发病率 已经超过了可比高收入国家的 发病率。这篇综述将 HPV 重新定义为一个紧迫的泌尿科、职业和公共卫生问题。主要借鉴 2023 年韩国性传播感染 (STI) 指南和 2021 年美国疾病控制与预防中心 STI 治疗 指南,并辅以从 PubMed 检索到的原始原始文献,我们解决了:(1) 男性特异性流行病学转变;(2)非性和职业性的传播途径,特别强调手术烟雾;(3)泌尿外科实践中循证感染控制;(4)肛门生殖器疣的临床治疗;(5)男性HPV 疫苗接种政策、国际比较及成本效益;(6) 未来的研究重点,包括 OPC 预防、HPV 和男性不育,以及泌尿科特定感染控制指南的需求。从 2026 年起,韩国将把 12 岁男孩纳入国家免疫计划,这是韩国首个性别中立的 HPV 疫苗接种 政策。这篇综述强调了泌尿科医师在 HPV 管理中不断扩大且不可或缺的作用,并强调感染控制警惕和性别中立的 疫苗接种 策略对于减少 HPV 相关疾病日益增加的社会负担至关重要。本综述为泌尿科医生提供了一个实用的框架,将 HPV 预防、感染控制和 疫苗接种 咨询纳入常规临床实践。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)长期以来被视为妇科病原体,但其在男性中的疾病负担却相当显著且迅速上升。
证据基础与方法
主要依据2023年韩国性传播疾病(STI)指南和2021年美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)性传播疾病人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)指南,并辅以从PubMed检索到的原始文献,我们探讨了以下内容:(1)男性特定的流行病学变化;(2)非性传播和职业传播途径,特别是手术烟雾;(3)基于证据的泌尿外科感染控制措施;(4)肛生殖器疣的临床管理;(5)男性人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)防控政策、国际比较及成本效益;(6)未来研究重点,包括口腔-咽喉癌(OPC)的预防、HPV与男性不育的关系,以及需要制定泌尿外科特定的感染控制指南。
共识与结论
本综述将人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)重新定位为泌尿外科、职业健康和公共卫生的重要问题。
争议与缺口
提供的证据中未报告具体的争议或证据缺口。
科研与实践启示
从2026年起,韩国将在其国家免疫计划中纳入12岁男孩——这是韩国首个性别中立的人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)防控政策。

Human Papillomavirus-Related Diseases in Urology: Diagnosis, Treatment, Prevention, and Clinical Considerations for Healthcare Providers

Authors: Kyu Won Lee, Amit Mani Upadhyay, Sangrak Bae
Original abstract (English)
Human papillomavirus (HPV) has long been regarded as a gynecologic pathogen, yet its disease burden in men is substantial and rapidly increasing. In Korea, the prevalence of anogenital warts in men has tripled over the past decade, penile cancer continues to rise, and HPV-positive oropharyngeal cancer (OPC) incidence in men has already exceeded that of cervical cancer in comparable high-income countries. This review reframes HPV as an urgent urological, occupational, and public health concern. Drawing primarily on the 2023 Korean Sexually Transmitted Infection (STI) Guidelines and the 2021 U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention STI Treatment Guidelines, supplemented by original primary literature retrieved from PubMed, we address: (1) the male-specific epidemiological shift; (2) nonsexual and occupational transmission routes, with particular emphasis on surgical smoke; (3) evidence-based infection control in urology practice; (4) clinical management of anogenital warts; (5) male HPV vaccination policy, international comparisons, and cost-effectiveness; and (6) future research priorities including OPC prevention, HPV and male infertility, and the need for urology-specific infection control guidelines. From 2026, Korea will include 12-year-old boys in its National Immunization Program—the first gender-neutral HPV vaccination policy in Korea. This review highlights the expanding and indispensable role of urologists in HPV management and emphasizes that infection control vigilance and gender-neutral vaccination strategies are essential to reduce the mounting societal burden of HPV-related diseases. This review provides a practical framework for urologists to integrate HPV prevention, infection control, and vaccination counseling into routine clinical practice.

尖锐湿疣中人乳头瘤病毒基因型分布的系统综述和荟萃分析:HIV阳性与HIV阴性患者中不同的分布特征

A systematic review and meta-analysis of human papillomavirus genotype distribution in condyloma acuminata: divergent profiles in HIV-positive and HIV-negative patients
作者： Yurike I. Pratiwi, Idrianti Idrus, Safruddin Amin
摘要中文翻译尖锐湿疣主要与低危型人乳头瘤病毒(LR-HPV)相关,但在HIV感染者中表现出更严重的临床过程,特征包括广泛病变、复发和对治疗的抵抗。该群体还表现出更高的高危型人乳头瘤病毒(HR-HPV)感染率以及与多种病毒的共感染,从而增加致癌风险。本综述旨在比较HIV阳性与HIV阴性患者中人乳头瘤病毒亚型的分布情况,以指导临床策略。进行了系统综述和荟萃分析,检索了PubMed、ScienceDirect、Cochrane/CENTRAL和Google Scholar等数据库中2015年至2025年发表的文献。共纳入6项研究,涉及703名参与者。使用RevMan 5.4.1中的随机效应模型对数据进行综合分析,以生成95%置信区间下的汇总比值比。通过ROBINS-I和SIGN工具评估方法学质量。结果显示,HIV状态是人乳头瘤病毒基因型分布的重要决定因素。与HIV阴性个体相比,HIV阳性个体中高危型人乳头瘤病毒(如基因型16、18、52、59)的感染频率显著更高,而HIV阴性个体中则以低危型人乳头瘤病毒为主(汇总比值比:5.25;95%置信区间:3.10–8.88;P <0.001)。总之,这些发现证实了HIV感染者中高危型人乳头瘤病毒的显著优势,强调了对该高危群体加强针对性筛查和预防性肿瘤学措施的必要性。

A systematic review and meta-analysis of human papillomavirus genotype distribution in condyloma acuminata: divergent profiles in HIV-positive and HIV-negative patients

Authors: Yurike I. Pratiwi, Idrianti Idrus, Safruddin Amin
Original abstract (English)
Condyloma acuminata, chiefly linked to low-risk human papillomavirus (LR-HPV), demonstrates a more severe clinical course – characterized by extensive lesions, recurrence, and treatment resistance–in HIV individuals. This group also shows increased high-risk HPV (HR-HPV) infection and coinfection prevalence, elevating oncogenic risk. This review aimed to compare HPV subtype profiles between HIV-positive and HIV-negative patients to guide clinical strategies. A systematic review and meta-analysis was conducted, querying PubMed, ScienceDirect, Cochrane/CENTRAL, and Google Scholar for literature published from 2015 to 2025. Six studies involving 703 participants were included. Data were synthesized using a random-effects model in RevMan 5.4.1 to generate pooled odds ratios with 95% confidence intervals. Methodological quality was evaluated via ROBINS-I and SIGN tools. Results demonstrated that HIV status was a significant determinant of HPV genotype distribution. Individuals living with HIV had substantially higher frequencies of HR-HPV infections (e.g. genotypes 16, 18, 52, 59) compared with HIV-negative individuals, in whom LR-HPV types predominated (pooled odds ratio: 5.25; 95% confidence interval: 3.10–8.88; P <0.001). In summary, these findings confirm a distinct HR-HPV predominance in individuals living with HIV, underscoring the imperative for enhanced, tailored screening, and preventive oncology measures in this high-risk group.

Intratumoral Human Papillomavirus in Advanced Prostate Cancer: Systematic Review, Meta-Analysis, and Real-World Evidence from the Largest Cohort to Date
作者： Daniele Brenna, Marialuisa Puglisi, Samantha Epistolio, Jessica Barizzi, Martino Pedrani, Giuseppe Salfi, Giovanna Pecoraro, Fabio Turco, Luigi Tortola, Salvatore Cozzi

Intratumoral Human Papillomavirus in Advanced Prostate Cancer: Systematic Review, Meta-Analysis, and Real-World Evidence from the Largest Cohort to Date

Authors: Daniele Brenna, Marialuisa Puglisi, Samantha Epistolio, Jessica Barizzi, Martino Pedrani, Giuseppe Salfi, Giovanna Pecoraro, Fabio Turco, Luigi Tortola, Salvatore Cozzi
Original abstract (English)
The etiopathogenesis of prostate cancer (PCa) is complex and involves multiple hormonal and environmental factors. Among these, the role of infectious agents remains controversial. Human papillomavirus (HPV) is known to promote carcinogenesis in epithelial tumors and has also been detected, albeit inconsistently, in PCa tissues. This systematic review, meta-analysis, and real-world cohort study aimed to clarify the prevalence and the prognostic role of intratumoral (IT)-HPV detection in locally advanced and metastatic PCa. We performed a systematic literature search including studies reporting IT-HPV detection in locally advanced (LA-PCa) and metastatic PCa (mPCa). Pooled prevalence estimates were calculated using random-effects models. In parallel, HPV DNA was analyzed using archival tissue in a single-center real-world retrospective cohort of 196 metastatic PCa patients. Twenty-three records met the inclusion criteria, comprising 22 full-text publications and one abstract. Nineteen datasets, encompassing 837 patients, contributed to the quantitative synthesis of stage III–IV disease. Pooled IT-HPV prevalence in mPCa was 18.5% (95% CI, 6.4–34%), while including also LA-PCa was 14.6% (95% CI, 5.2–26.8). Eastern countries showed higher IT-HPV prevalence in both mPCa and LA-PCa (40% and 34.3%, respectively) compared with Western regions (0.8% and 0.3%; p 7) in advanced stage (pooled OR 6.32, 95% CI, 3.13–12.78) compared with IT-HPV-negative PCa; however, no studies reported data on its association with survival outcomes. Within our retrospective cohort, IT-HPV DNA was detected in 3.1% of cases (6/196) and exhibited typical PCa driver alterations, such as TP53, SPOP, PIK3CA, and AR amplification. IT–HPV seems to be infrequent in metastatic and locally advanced PCa in Western populations and more prevalent in Asian cohorts and might be associated with a higher Gleason score. IT–HPV-positive advanced PCa seems to exhibit typical PCa driver alterations. Data on the association between IT–HPV status and survival outcomes in PCa are lacking. Standardized molecular assays and multicenter prospective studies are needed to delineate its significance in PCa.
View review five-element analysis

Evidence base and method

Consensus and conclusions
Eastern countries showed higher IT-HPV prevalence in both mPCa and LA-PCa (40% and 34.3%, respectively) compared with Western regions (0.8% and 0.3%; p 7) in advanced stage (pooled OR 6.32, 95% CI, 3.13–12.78) compared with IT-HPV-negative PCa; however, no studies reported data on its association with survival outcomes.
Controversies and gaps
IT–HPV seems to be infrequent in metastatic and locally advanced PCa in Western populations and more prevalent in Asian cohorts and might be associated with a higher Gleason score.
Research and practice implications
Data on the association between IT–HPV status and survival outcomes in PCa are lacking.

美国农村青少年中多层级阻碍人乳头瘤病毒疫苗接种的分析

An Analysis of Multilevel Barriers to Human Papillomavirus Vaccination Uptake Among Rural U.S. Adolescents
作者： Tajauna Batchelor, Madison Brown, Kimbrionna Hunter, Asma Hanif, Shumaila Nida Javed Tunio
摘要中文翻译尽管人乳头瘤病毒疫苗(HPV)长期可获得,但其仍然是美国最常见的性传播感染,并且是可预防癌症的主要原因之一。此外,HPV疫苗接种率仍低于其他常规推荐的青少年疫苗接种率,尤其是在农村人群中。本研究旨在识别影响农村社区HPV疫苗接种的与医疗保健获取、社会经济状况、文化信念以及提供者-患者沟通相关的障碍。通过回顾2020年至2025年间发表的英文文章,并分析国家数据集,以确定HPV疫苗接种率的趋势。来自美国健康排名和美国疾病控制与预防中心青少年疫苗接种调查的数据显示,13至17岁青少年的HPV疫苗接种率在州级层面被获取。根据农村-城市连续性代码,将各州分类为以农村为主或以城市为主,并比较其平均疫苗接种完成率。农村州的平均HPV疫苗接种完成率为60.55%,而城市州为67.31%;然而,这一差异未达到所选统计显著性阈值(p = 0.025)。文献中识别出的障碍包括医疗保健获取减少、提供者沟通差异、社会经济限制以及农村社区健康素养有限。在这些识别出的障碍中,医疗保健提供者的推荐是预测HPV疫苗接种接受度最强的预测因素之一。这些发现突显了多层级决定因素对HPV疫苗接种差异的影响,并强调了需要针对未被充分服务的青少年群体,采取有针对性的、基于证据的策略以提高HPV疫苗接种的可及性和覆盖率。

An Analysis of Multilevel Barriers to Human Papillomavirus Vaccination Uptake Among Rural U.S. Adolescents

Authors: Tajauna Batchelor, Madison Brown, Kimbrionna Hunter, Asma Hanif, Shumaila Nida Javed Tunio
Original abstract (English)
Despite longstanding vaccine availability, human papillomavirus (HPV) remains the most common sexually transmitted infection in the United States and a leading cause of preventable cancers. Additionally, HPV vaccination rates remain below other routinely recommended adolescent immunizations, particularly in rural populations. This study aimed to identify barriers related to healthcare access, socioeconomic conditions, cultural beliefs, and provider–patient communication in rural communities. A bibliographical review of articles published in English from 2020 to 2025 and an analysis of national datasets were conducted to establish trends in HPV vaccination rates. State-level HPV vaccination data for adolescents aged 13–17 years were obtained from America’s Health Rankings and the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention National Immunization Survey-Teen. States were classified as predominantly rural or urban using Rural–Urban Continuum Codes, and mean vaccination completion rates were compared. The mean HPV vaccination completion rate was lower in rural states (60.55%) compared to urban states (67.31%); however, this difference did not meet the selected threshold for statistical significance (p = 0.025). Barriers identified in the literature included reduced access to healthcare, differences in provider communication, socioeconomic constraints, and limited health literacy in rural communities. Of the identified barriers, healthcare provider recommendations emerged as one of the strongest predictors of vaccine acceptance. These findings highlight multilevel determinants contributing to differences in HPV vaccine uptake and underscore the need for targeted, evidence-based strategies to improve vaccine access and coverage in underserved adolescent populations.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study aimed to identify barriers related to healthcare access, socioeconomic conditions, cultural beliefs, and provider–patient communication in rural communities that affect HPV vaccination uptake.
Evidence base and method
A bibliographical review of articles published in English from 2020 to 2025 and an analysis of national datasets were conducted to establish trends in HPV vaccination rates. State-level HPV vaccination data for adolescents aged 13–17 years were obtained from America’s Health Rankings and the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention National Immunization Survey-Teen. States were classified as predominantly rural or urban using Rural–Urban Continuum Codes, and mean vaccination completion rates were compared.
Consensus and conclusions
The mean HPV vaccination completion rate was lower in rural states (60.55%) compared to urban states (67.31%); however, this difference did not meet the selected threshold for statistical significance (p = 0.025). Of the identified barriers, healthcare provider recommendations emerged as one of the strongest predictors of vaccine acceptance.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report on specific controversies or evidence gaps related to HPV vaccination uptake in rural communities.
Research and practice implications
These findings highlight multilevel determinants contributing to differences in HPV vaccine uptake and underscore the need for targeted, evidence-based strategies to improve vaccine access and coverage in underserved adolescent populations.

一种以性别为导向的方法用于人乳头瘤病毒 疫苗接种:对临床前医学学生的横断面研究

A Gender-Oriented Approach to Human Papillomavirus Vaccination: A Cross-Sectional Study Among Preclinical Medical Students
作者： Yüksel Duygu Altıparmak, Tümay Sadıkoğlu, Ülkü Sur Ünal, Tumay Sadikoglu
摘要中文翻译目标:对抗宫颈癌的第一道防线是人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)疫苗接种。人们普遍认为HPV 疫苗接种主要针对女性,这阻碍了对抗HPV的进展。本研究旨在探讨临床前医学学生对当前HPV 疫苗接种项目的性别视角,并评估性别导向方法在HPV 宫颈癌预防项目中的潜在影响及其对HPV 疫苗接种建议知识的评估。材料与方法:本描述性横断面研究评估了临床前医学学生对HPV 疫苗接种建议的了解程度。HPV知识量表被用于评估符合条件的临床前学生。不完整的问卷被排除在分析之外。结果:共分析了448份问卷,对应88.3%的回复率。关于HPV 疫苗对11至26岁男性推荐的识别,仅34.8%的男性学生(n=46)和31.0%的女性学生(n=98)正确识别了这一适应症。对于11至26岁女性,正确回答的比例更高,分别为60.6%(n=80)的男性学生和69.3%(n=219)的女性学生。结论:提高医学学生对疫苗接种项目的了解是实现一级预防的重要步骤,这是一公共卫目标。我们的研究结果表明,临床前医学学生对男性HPV 疫苗接种建议的了解程度低于对女性的了解,这表明有必要加强本科医学教育中性别中立的HPV 疫苗接种内容。争议与证据空白:我们的研究结果表明,临床前医学学生对男性HPV 疫苗接种建议的了解程度低于对女性的了解,这表明有必要加强本科医学教育中性别中立的HPV 疫苗接种内容。研究与实践意义:人们普遍认为HPV 疫苗接种主要针对女性,这阻碍了对抗HPV的进展。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
针对宫颈癌的第一道防线是人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)疫苗接种。本研究旨在探讨临床医学预科生对当前HPV 疫苗接种项目的性别视角,并评估基于性别的方法对宫颈癌预防项目对其HPV 疫苗接种建议知识的潜在影响。
证据基础与方法
材料与方法:本描述性横断面研究评估了预临床医学学生的HPV 疫苗接种建议知晓情况。将HPV知识量表发放给符合条件的预临床学生。不完整的问卷被排除在分析之外。
共识与结论
共分析了448份问卷,对应88.3%的回复率。关于推荐高危型HPV疫苗(疫苗)用于11–26岁男性,仅34.8%的男性学生(n=46)和31.0%的女性学生(n=98)正确识别了这一适应症。对于11–26岁女性,正确回答的比例更高,分别为60.6%(n=80)的男性学生和69.3%(n=219)的女性学生。提高医学生对疫苗接种项目的了解是实现一级预防的重要步骤,这是一公共卫生实践的关键目标。
争议与缺口
我们的研究结果表明,临床前医学学生对男性HPV 疫苗接种建议的知晓率低于对女性的知晓率,这表明亟需加强本科医学教育中性别中立的HPV 疫苗接种内容。
科研与实践启示
认为人乳头瘤病毒 疫苗接种 主要针对女性的观念阻碍了对抗 HPV 的进展。

A Gender-Oriented Approach to Human Papillomavirus Vaccination: A Cross-Sectional Study Among Preclinical Medical Students

Authors: Yüksel Duygu Altıparmak, Tümay Sadıkoğlu, Ülkü Sur Ünal, Tumay Sadikoglu
Original abstract (English)
Objective: The first line of defense against cervical cancer is human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination. The perception that HPV vaccination is primarily intended for women hinders progress in the fight against HPV. This study aimed to examine preclinical medical students’ gender-based perspectives on the current HPV vaccination program and to assess the potential impact of gender-based approaches to cervical cancer prevention programs on their knowledge of HPV vaccination recommendations. Materials and Methods: This descriptive cross-sectional study assessed preclinical medical students’ awareness of HPV vaccination recommendations. The HPV Knowledge Scale was administered to eligible preclinical students. Incomplete questionnaires were excluded from the analysis. Results: A total of 448 questionnaires were analyzed, corresponding to an 88.3% response rate. Regarding the recommendation of the HPV vaccine for males aged 11–26 years, only 34.8% of male students (n=46) and 31.0% of female students (n=98) correctly identified this indication. For females aged 11–26 years, the proportion of correct responses was higher, at 60.6% (n=80) among male students and 69.3% (n=219) among female students. Conclusion: Improving medical students’ knowledge of vaccination programs is an important step toward achieving primary prevention, a key objective of public health practice. Our findings demonstrate that preclinical medical students have lower awareness of HPV vaccination recommendations for males than for females, indicating an urgent need to strengthen gender-neutral HPV vaccination content in undergraduate medical ­education.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The first line of defense against cervical cancer is human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination. This study aimed to examine preclinical medical students’ gender-based perspectives on the current HPV vaccination program and to assess the potential impact of gender-based approaches to cervical cancer prevention programs on their knowledge of HPV vaccination recommendations.
Evidence base and method
Materials and Methods: This descriptive cross-sectional study assessed preclinical medical students’ awareness of HPV vaccination recommendations. The HPV Knowledge Scale was administered to eligible preclinical students. Incomplete questionnaires were excluded from the analysis.
Consensus and conclusions
A total of 448 questionnaires were analyzed, corresponding to an 88.3% response rate. Regarding the recommendation of the HPV vaccine for males aged 11–26 years, only 34.8% of male students (n=46) and 31.0% of female students (n=98) correctly identified this indication. For females aged 11–26 years, the proportion of correct responses was higher, at 60.6% (n=80) among male students and 69.3% (n=219) among female students. Improving medical students’ knowledge of vaccination programs is an important step toward achieving primary prevention, a key objective of public health practice.
Controversies and gaps
Our findings demonstrate that preclinical medical students have lower awareness of HPV vaccination recommendations for males than for females, indicating an urgent need to strengthen gender-neutral HPV vaccination content in undergraduate medical education.
Research and practice implications
The perception that HPV vaccination is primarily intended for women hinders progress in the fight against HPV.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-09-02  |  Publisher: Bing News RSS
B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

HPV感染可能略微增加新发心脏病和死亡风险

HPV infection may confer slightly higher risk of new-onset heart disease and death
新闻简报A study published in PLOS Medicine suggests that HPV infection may slightly increase the risk of new-onset cardiovascular disease (CVD) and death in women, particularly within the first year of infection. The research, led by Karolinska Institutet, compared 497,445 HPV-infected women with 2.5 million uninfected controls and found a 7% higher risk of CVD and a 25% higher risk of death in infected women. In the first year, the risks were 43% and 86% higher, respectively, but declined afterward. The study also compared 143,787 infected women with their 174,637 unexposed siblings, yielding similar results. The authors advise clinicians to be aware of the slightly elevated CVD risk in HPV-infected women but emphasize that the small absolute difference does not warrant undue concern. No serious adverse events were recorded, and the findings are based on Swedish national registries.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026-09-02发表,研究结果基于瑞典国家注册数据,并随访期长达感染后一年。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于瑞典女性,比较了HPV感染女性与未感染对照组以及其未暴露的兄弟姐妹。
事件
一项发表在《PLOS Medicine》上的研究指出,HPV感染可能略微增加女性新发心血管疾病(CVD)和死亡的风险,尤其是在随访的第一年内。
规模、影响与风险
人乳头瘤病毒感染女性发生新发心血管疾病的风险比未感染女性高7%,绝对发病率差异为每1,000人年0.53例。感染女性的死亡风险比未感染女性高25%,绝对发病率差异为每100,000人年6.71例。在第一年,心血管疾病和死亡的风险分别比未感染女性高43%和86%,但之后逐渐下降。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究为观察性研究,强调临床医生应意识到HPV感染女性在感染后第一年内略微升高的CVD风险。作者指出,这种微小的绝对差异意味着女性不应过度担忧。研究结果基于瑞典的注册数据和兄弟姐妹比较,未记录到与HPV感染有因果关系的严重不良事件。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HPV infection may confer slightly higher risk of new-onset heart disease and death

News Brief
A study published in PLOS Medicine suggests that HPV infection may slightly increase the risk of new-onset cardiovascular disease (CVD) and death in women, particularly within the first year of infection. The research, led by Karolinska Institutet, compared 497,445 HPV-infected women with 2.5 million uninfected controls and found a 7% higher risk of CVD and a 25% higher risk of death in infected women. In the first year, the risks were 43% and 86% higher, respectively, but declined afterward. The study also compared 143,787 infected women with their 174,637 unexposed siblings, yielding similar results. The authors advise clinicians to be aware of the slightly elevated CVD risk in HPV-infected women but emphasize that the small absolute difference does not warrant undue concern. No serious adverse events were recorded, and the findings are based on Swedish national registries.
Original source text (English)

Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search CLOSE Search Support Menu HPV infection may confer slightly higher risk of new-onset heart disease and death News brief Mary Van Beusekom, MS Topics Human Papillomavirus (HPV) Share Copied to clipboard Chinnapong / iStock Women infected with human papillomavirus (HPV) are at higher risk for new-onset cardiovascular disease (CVD) and death than their uninfected peers, a Karolinska Institutet–led research team suggests. For the observational study, published yesterday in PLOS Medicine, the researchers compared the CVD diagnoses of 497,445 HPV-infected women in Swedish national registries with those of roughly 2.5 million uninfected controls. They also compared CVD status among 143,787 infected women and their 174,637 unexposed full siblings. The authors note that most previous observational research was limited to specific populations and didn’t adequately consider familial factors. HPV infection is well known to cause cervical cancer, but emerging evidence suggests that it may also contribute to CVD. Sibling comparison yields similar findings New-onset CVD was identified in 28,793 and 128,373 HPV-infected and uninfected women, respectively, for respective incidences of 8.27 and 7.93 per 1,000 person-years. In total, 1,221 and 4,941 women with and without HPV infection died of CVD, for respective death rates of 33.7 and 29.4 per 100,000 person-years. The risks of CVD and death were greatest within the first year of follow-up, then waned. The findings suggested that clinicians should be aware of a slightly elevated CVD risk in women with HPV, especially during the first year following an infection. The risk of new-onset CVD was 7% higher in HPV-infected women than in their uninfected counterparts, for an absolute rate difference of 0.53 cases per 1,000 person-years (population-attributable fraction, 1.16%). The risk of death was 25% higher in infected women than in unexposed women, for an absolute rate difference of 6.71 cases per 100,000 person-years (population-attributable fraction, 4.12%). In the first year of follow-up, the risks of incident CVD and related death were 43% and 86% higher, respectively, in women diagnosed as having HPV. But thereafter, the excess risk fell to 2% for new-onset CVD and 21% for related death. In the sibling comparison, HPV-infected women had a 5% greater risk of incident CVD and a 25% higher risk of death than their uninfected siblings. “The findings suggested that clinicians should be aware of a slightly elevated CVD risk in women with HPV, especially during the first year following an infection,” the authors concluded. “However, given the small absolute difference, women should not be unduly concerned.” Monoclonal antibody shows promise against dengue viremia, fever in phase 2 trial News brief Chris Dall, MA Topics Dengue Monica R / Flickr cc An experimental monoclonal antibody (mAb) for dengue virus was safe and well-tolerated and provided evidence of a therapeutic effect in a phase 2 clinical trial, researchers reported last week in JAMA Network Open. In the randomized, placebo-controlled trial, a team led by investigators from Serum Institute of India assigned 250 adults with dengue and a history of fever onset within 48 hours to receive four different dose levels of Dengue-mAb (3, 5, 7, or 9 milligrams per kilogram [mg/kg]) or a placebo, with 50 participants assigned to each group. The primary outcomes were reduction in viremia at 24 hours and causally related serious adverse events (SAEs). Dengue-mAb is a human-engineered, recombinant monoclonal antibody that has neutralized all four serotypes of dengue virus in animal studies and demonstrated safety in a phase 1 trial in healthy adults. Dengue virus affects more than 200 million people each year and is expanding into new areas amid climate change and rapid urbanization. In addition, the incidence of severe disease from the fast-spreading mosquito-borne virus is rising. While there are three currently licensed vaccines for dengue, there are no licensed antiviral drugs. “Improvements in case management to reduce the risk of severe dengue are still needed,” the investigators wrote. Rapid clearance of viremia, fever Treatment with Dengue-mAb demonstrated a rapid clearance of dengue viremia and fever within 24 hours at all dose levels compared with placebo. Participants with detectable viremia at baseline were negative for dengue virus by eight hours when treated with the 5- to 9-mg/kg dose of Dengue-mAb, compared with 72 hours with placebo. At 24 hours, fever clearance ranged from 92.9% to 100% in the Dengue-mAb groups, compared with 58.3% in the placebo group. This is, to our knowledge, the first evidence of a preliminary therapeutic effect in patients with dengue. No causally related SAEs were recorded. “This first-in-patient phase 2 randomized clinical trial demonstrated the safety of Dengue-mAb and its action on the clearance of dengue virus and fever,” the authors wrote. “This is, to our knowledge, the first evidence of a preliminary therapeutic effect in patients with dengue.” In case you missed it Previous Next Quick takes: Cyclosporiasis in Michigan, Aedes mosquitoes in the UK, new NYC Legionnaires measures Regular exercise before COVID infection linked to better daily function 2 years later Study details deaths, anaphylaxis linked to antibiotic injections Ebola case counts reach 6,250 in DR Congo outbreak Lancaster County coroner confirms that second measles death also involves a child Rotavirus vaccine shows strong global results in preschoolers Antiviral drug remdesivir may cut risk of death in adults with severe COVID and kidney or liver disease Quick takes: Schools resume in DR Congo; sprouts, mangos recalled; 2 H9N2 avian flu cases in China This week's top reads Newborn one of 2 measles-associated deaths in Pennsylvania The baby died of a laceration of the spleen, which may have been caused by a measles infection in the mother. Time
The study was published on 2026-09-02, and the findings were based on data from Swedish national registries and follow-up periods up to one year post-infection.
Location and population
The study focused on women in Sweden, comparing those infected with HPV with uninfected controls and their unexposed full siblings.
Event
A research study published in PLOS Medicine suggests that HPV infection may confer a slightly higher risk of new-onset cardiovascular disease (CVD) and death, particularly within the first year of follow-up.
Scale, impact and risk
The risk of new-onset CVD was 7% higher in HPV-infected women than in uninfected counterparts, with an absolute rate difference of 0.53 cases per 1,000 person-years. The risk of death was 25% higher in infected women, with an absolute rate difference of 6.71 cases per 100,000 person-years. In the first year, the risks were 43% and 86% higher for CVD and death, respectively, but declined afterward.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study is observational and highlights the need for clinicians to be aware of the slightly elevated CVD risk in women with HPV, especially during the first year post-infection. The authors note that the small absolute difference means women should not be unduly concerned. The findings are based on Swedish registries and sibling comparisons, with no causally related serious adverse events recorded.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

HPV 疫苗在美国青少年中的接种率在4年内未增加

HPV vaccine uptake in US teens hasn’t increased in 4 years
新闻简报该报告指出,美国13至17岁青少年中HPV 疫苗的接种率在过去四年中没有增加。根据2025年国家免疫接种调查-青少年报告,78.2%的青少年至少接种了一剂HPV 疫苗,62.9%完成了接种系列。这一比例低于健康目标2030设定的80%的完整疫苗接种接种目标。CDC作者认为,较低的接种覆盖率可能是由于较少的州将HPV 疫苗接种纳入学校入学要求,与Tdap和MenACWY 疫苗相比。他们建议解决疫苗接种接种的障碍,包括改善预防性护理的可及性、加强提供者的沟通以及提高疫苗接种的信心。报告还指出,接受MMR 疫苗两剂的青少年比例为92.5%,低于健康目标2030设定的95%。这些发现强调了继续努力提高青少年HPV 疫苗接种接种率的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
报告指出,美国青少年中人乳头瘤病毒 疫苗 的接种率在4年内没有上升,数据反映的是2024年的覆盖率。还提到了2025年的调查数据,但与2023年相比,2024年Tdap和MenACWY的接种率有所下降。该报告的发表日期是2026-09-04。
地点与对象
该报告聚焦于美国,特别是13至17岁的青少年群体。它还提到了美国20多岁的女性群体以及宾夕法尼亚州的一般公众。
事件
事件是美国青少年HPV 疫苗接种率在过去四年中没有增加,如《发病率和死亡率周报》所报道。CDC作者指出,与其他常规青少年疫苗相比,HPV 疫苗接种接种覆盖率较低,可能是由于学校入学要求存在差异。
规模、影响与风险
2024年,美国13至17岁青少年中,78.2%至少接种了一剂HPV 疫苗,62.9%完成了接种系列。接受MMR 疫苗两剂的青少年比例为92.5%,低于健康目标2030设定的95%。该报告强调了继续努力提高接种覆盖率,尤其是HPV接种率的必要性。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC作者建议解决疫苗接种接种的障碍,包括改善预防性护理的可及性、加强提供者的沟通以及提高疫苗接种的信心。报告还提到了健康目标2030以及继续提高疫苗接种接种率的必要性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HPV vaccine uptake in US teens hasn’t increased in 4 years

News Brief
The report highlights that HPV vaccine uptake among US teenagers aged 13 to 17 has not increased over the past four years. According to the 2025 National Immunization Survey–Teen, 78.2% of teens received at least one dose of the HPV vaccine, and 62.9% completed the series. This is lower than the Healthy People 2030 goal of 80% full vaccination. The CDC authors suggest that the lower coverage might be due to fewer states requiring HPV vaccination compared to Tdap and MenACWY vaccines. They recommend addressing barriers to vaccination, including improving access to preventive care, strengthening provider communication, and increasing vaccine confidence. The report also notes that the percentage of teens receiving both doses of the MMR vaccine was 92.5%, below the 95% Healthy People 2030 target. These findings underscore the need for continued efforts to enhance HPV vaccination rates among adolescents.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Main navigation News Antimicrobial Resistance COVID-19 Chronic Wasting Disease Influenza Public Health Alerts All Topics A-Z Coronavirus Vaccines Roadmap Influenza Vaccines Roadmap Roadmaps for Priority Pathogens Vaccine Integrity Project CIDRAP Leadership Forum . . Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search CLOSE Search Support Menu HPV vaccine uptake in US teens hasn’t increased in 4 years Meghan Holohan Childhood Vaccines Diphtheria Human Papillomavirus (HPV) Pertussis nd3000 / iStock Share Copied to clipboard For the fourth year in a row, the number of US teenagers being vaccinated against human papillomavirus (HPV) has not increased, according to survey results published yesterday in Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report. A smaller percentage of adolescents received the HPV series compared with recipients of the tetanus, diphtheria, and pertussis (whooping cough) vaccine (Tdap) and the conjugate meningococcal vaccine against the A, C, W, and Y strains (Men ACWY). But uptake of these immunizations declined slightly from last year. “The consistently lower HPV vaccination coverage compared with other routine adolescent vaccines might, in part, reflect differences in school entry requirements,” wrote the authors, from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). “Tdap is required for school entry in all states, and MenACWY is required in most states; however, few states require HPV vaccination.” Need to address barriers to vaccination The researchers examined data from the 2025 National Immunization Survey–Teen to understand vaccination rates in teens aged 13 to 17 years. Children aged 11 to 12 should receive a Tdap, MenACWY, and HPV vaccine (though the HPV series can be started at 9 years old), per the 2025 CDC Child and Adolescent Immunization Schedule. Last year, the number of teens aged 13 to 17 who received at least one dose each of Tdap and MenACWY dropped from 2024. For Tdap, the percentage of teens receiving at least one dose declined from 91.3% to 88.8%. When it came to MenACWY, the percentage dipped from 90.1% to 89.0%. While the recent survey stated the number of teens receiving the HPV vaccine held steady from the previous four years, it did not include those data. But last year’s survey, which reflects vaccine coverage data for 2024, showed that 78.2% of teens aged 13 to 17 received one dose or more of the HPV vaccine, and 62.9% completed the series. Efforts to address barriers to vaccination, including improving access to preventive care, strengthening provider communication regarding recommendations, and increasing vaccine confidence, could help improve coverage. In 2025, 63.4% of teens in the same age-group received the entire HPV vaccine series, far lower than the Healthy People 2030 goal of 80% being fully vaccinated. And the portion receiving at least one dose dropped by a half percentage point from the year before, to 77.7%. The percentage of teens 13 to 17 who received both doses of the measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine (MMR) vaccine was 92.5%, also lower than the 95% Healthy People 2030 goal. “Continued efforts are needed to increase adolescent vaccination coverage with recommended vaccines and reduce geographic differences in coverage, particularly with HPV vaccine,” the authors wrote. “Efforts to address barriers to vaccination, including improving access to preventive care, strengthening provider communication regarding recommendations, and increasing vaccine confidence, could help improve coverage.” Related news Previous Next Vaccine recommendations and access, plus measles deaths: 3 questions, zero consensus: The State of US Vaccine Policy Izzy Brandstetter Figueroa, PhD, MPH, Riley Mulholland, MPH, Jess Steier, DrPH About one-third of US women in their 20s have never been screened for cervical cancer Meghan Holohan Newborn one of 2 measles-associated deaths in Pennsylvania Sarah Boden Studies provide more evidence that HPV vaccines benefit men Liz Szabo CIDRAP Op-Ed: What a package insert actually says, and how it was turned against vaccines Jake Scott, MD Catholic bishops tell Florida AG there's no theological reason for school vaccine exemptions Sarah Boden People vaccinated against HPV have lower risk of head and neck cancer, study suggests Liz Szabo CIDRAP Op-Ed: No, 3 vaccines are not better than 1 when it comes to MMR Jess Steier, DrPh This week's top reads Newborn one of 2 measles-associated deaths in Pennsylvania The baby died of a laceration of the spleen, which may have been caused by a measles infection in the mother.
